([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwp-hwpx-mime-type-whitelist/1641))\n\nSo, if you are inspecting the *internal* `mimetype` file contained in an `.hwpx` container, Hancom’s forum answer points to `application/hwp+zip`, not `application/haansofthwpx`.\n\n---\n\n### 3) Hancom also says the HWPX MIME type is not officially registered; suggests `application/hwp+zip` (custom) or `application/octet-stream`\nIn a separate Hancom Developer Forum thread specifically about HTTP `Content-Type` headers for `.hwpx`, the same representative states:\n\n- “**hwpx는 minetype이 공식 등록되어 있지 않습니다**” (HWPX’s MIME type is not officially registered)\n- If you have a client-side “custom agreed behavior,” you can set **`Content-Type: application/hwp+zip`**\n- Otherwise you can use **`application/octet-stream`** (since it is a ZIP binary). ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwpx-http-content-type-application-hwp-zip/1752))\n\nThis helps explain why you might see multiple MIME strings in the wild for the same `.hwpx` files: different ecosystems choose different conventions.\n\n---\n\n### 4) Hancom’s technical write-up describes HWPX as a ZIP-based package of XML files, and confirms a `mimetype` file exists as a “signature”\nHancom Tech’s article on the HWPX format structure states:\n\n- HWPX is **XML files organized in a ZIP structure**\n- The **`mimetype` file** is used as **signature information** to confirm the file is HWPX. ([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))\n\nThis reinforces the “container + internal mimetype marker” concept, similar to how some other ZIP-based document formats include identifying files.\n\n---\n\n### 5) Background: MIME types are conveyed via HTTP `Content-Type`\nGeneral references (MDN) explain that MIME/media types are used in HTTP via the `Content-Type` header, and correct configuration matters for how clients interpret downloads.
